# Masullo formalization ledger

Open obligations are tracked in [GAPS.MD](GAPS.MD). Verified repairs and their scope are in [OLD_GAPS.MD](OLD_GAPS.MD).

Source is the 940-page supplied edition of *The Music of the Primes*. The numbers below are printed page numbers. “Mapped” means the section’s role is identified. “Modeled” means Lean contains a proposition with some analytic objects supplied as parameters. “Checked” means Lean verified the indicated implication or obstruction. A row may contain work at more than one level.

## Checking the current files

From the workspace root:

```sh
cd corpora/mathlib
lake +lean4-search-4.34-nightly env bash -c '
set -e
build_dir=$(mktemp -d /tmp/masullo-modules-XXXXXX)
trap '\''rm -rf "$build_dir"'\'' EXIT
export LEAN_PATH="$build_dir:$LEAN_PATH"
mkdir -p "$build_dir/External/PrimeNumberTheoremAnd"
lean --root=../../Masullo -o "$build_dir/Ideles.olean" ../../Masullo/Ideles.lean
lean --root=../../Masullo -o "$build_dir/GammaStripBounds.olean" ../../Masullo/GammaStripBounds.lean
```

The files pass these checks. The axiom audit of the main lemmas reported only propext, Classical.choice, and Quot.sound.

The workspace’s Mathlib build contains artifacts from the installed nightly and the local search toolchain. The local search toolchain reads this cache. The default nightly rejected dependencies with incompatible headers.

The half-open representatives are Borel, and restricting Haar measure to them and pushing forward gives Haar measure on norm-one classes. The total mass is the volume of the representative region. This supports the Chapter 11 transversal integral, with the paper’s numerical Tamagawa normalization tracked separately. [IdelicFundamentalMeasure.lean](IdelicFundamentalMeasure.lean)

## Measuring the manuscript coverage target

A row in the completion register counts once when Lean proves the full source result or an explicitly corrected replacement. Definitions, examples, partial statements, modeled interfaces, and counterexamples by themselves earn no credit.

The original planning denominator is approximately 598. Layout-preserving extraction, raw text extraction, and a check of positioned PDF text all identify the same 591 distinct four-part numbers headed Theorem, Proposition, Lemma, Corollary, or Claim. All verified rows match those headings. Several additional numbers found by searching within lines occur only as references, including the reference to “Proposition 4.2.2.7”, which points to an equation. Full coverage still requires reconciling the seven-count difference with the original estimate and checking for headings missed by extraction.

## Work order

For full coverage, use the completion register to identify uncovered source numbers and group them by shared dependencies. Defer the large unstated results, including Theorems 12.5.1.1 and 13.3.1.1, until the other numbered results have verified proofs or verified corrected replacements. Prioritize full statements that can reuse the verified structural and analytic layers. Review each new external proof against the local source statement and audit its transitive axioms before adding a completion row. Record partial work and counterexamples in the gap report until the remaining clauses or a full replacement check.

For Appendix C, all four conditions of C.2.4.2 now check. The proof uses two Hilbert factors for the forward gauge-transition series, shows a nuclear series defines an integral operator for the converse, and uses the Banach dual of the gauge completion to recover nuclearity from the completed comparison with Banach factors. [NuclearCriteria.lean](NuclearCriteria.lean) · [NuclearIntegralOperators.lean](NuclearIntegralOperators.lean) C.2.4.16 and C.2.4.18 now use those transitions to prove the approximation and scalar bilinear kernel theorems. C.2.4.19 still needs the completed tensor comparison for the strong dual of the actual Schwartz–Bruhat space and the structural properties used in D.1.5.7. [NuclearApproximation.lean](NuclearApproximation.lean) · [SchwartzKernel.lean](SchwartzKernel.lean)

C.3.6.4 now checks in full, including the projective tensor isometry onto the trace class. The projective norm is characterized by its elementary-tensor bound and by a norming bilinear form constructed from the singular families. The completed map lifts trace-class operators by summing their singular tensors. [HilbertOperatorTensors.lean](HilbertOperatorTensors.lean) · [ProjectiveOperatorTensors.lean](ProjectiveOperatorTensors.lean)

For Chapter 12, the corrected general trace and approximation theorem 12.1.1.2 is available. Apply it to the actual global idelic kernels in 12.1.1.3 by verifying the nuclear hypotheses and connecting their smoothing to the local summands and the trace formula. [WeightedKernelDiagonal.lean](WeightedKernelDiagonal.lean) [KernelBasisCutoffs.lean](KernelBasisCutoffs.lean) [GAPS.MD](GAPS.MD#g4-the-local-computation-lacks-the-global-assembly-and-scope-checks)

The external refresh identified verified proof ingredients for the next ports. The three Dynin–Mityagin clauses of 4.1.2.6 now check using the local basis definitions and a finite-sum estimate proved directly from nuclear tensor topology. The local port of 9.1.1.3 now verifies all three embeddings on the completed spaces. Appendix D now supplies uniqueness of the normalized measure and the unique unitary extension once the norm identity holds. Corrected L¹ inversion and quotient Poisson summation check with that normalization. Proving that a compatible Haar normalization exists in general remains open. The S-local homogeneous theta integrability argument can be reused when completing the other clauses of 2.3.1.4.

For the coverage target, continue the adelic theta estimates in Chapter 2 from the lattice and one-parameter results. The general integrable-input Weil theorem is available for Fourier analysis on quotient groups. The Fourier and Schwartz–Bruhat arguments can build on the verified translation and strip estimates, and the vector-valued contour arguments can build on the checked Pettis theory. Other work toward the checkpoint includes operator-ideal estimates, Mellin continuation and Poisson summation for piecewise smooth tests, permanence and inertia results from the corrected embedding criterion, and arithmetic quotients in Appendix A.

1. Keep the real-place obstruction, the reference-subtraction repair, and the final conditional lemmas checked. Fix the intended versions of the main trace and divisor statements before building their dependencies. The gap report identifies the required manuscript inputs. [GAPS.MD](GAPS.MD)
2. Connect the existing norm-variable Mellin test space and Mathlib Dirichlet L-functions to the actual idele-class trace over ℚ. The primitive-character reflection is checked. Prove the character projection and spell out the Euler and archimedean distributions.
3. Build on the idele class group and the algebraic Schwartz–Bruhat space. Prove local compactness and the appropriate quotient and Haar results, then the convergence and Hecke L-function continuation. Formalize the independent classical explicit formula.
4. Work toward the trace in two branches. On the spectral side identify the quotient inside weighted L² and prove a summable majorant for the rank-one norms over characters, zeros and multiplicities. On the geometric side verify smoothed diagonal extraction, the local computation, and the shell series limit. Match the theorem’s support and regularity assumptions at the join.
5. Prove the divisor step on the intended common test space and then instantiate critical-strip GRH, with the necessary nonvanishing and functional-equation facts. Formalize the remaining complements and background results for full manuscript coverage.

The trace interface takes the transform and geometric terms as inputs. Its Lean proofs therefore certify implications about trace identities, including convergence via HasSum. They do not establish any of the analytic trace identities on the idele class group. The Dirichlet GRH declaration is a proposition over Mathlib objects. The general GRH declaration takes a family of continued L-functions as an argument.

The `a9c1be1` external refresh led to the checked repair of 5.3.1.1 and the cutoff measurability port. The weak Cauchy and Taylor proofs are verified external. Their local topology comparisons and the two contour clauses that depend on placeholders external remain open.

The verified small-modulus logarithmic estimate now uses the local translated S-unit count and the actual S-local test topology. It gives both weighted boundedness and vanishing at zero, including the compact norm-one character averages. The large-modulus estimates and directional derivatives of these same orbit sums now also check. These estimates apply to the S-local Mellin integrals in Chapter 3. [Orbit sums](SLocalThetaGrowth.lean) · [Small-modulus bounds](SLocalThetaSmallNorm.lean)

For Theorem 3.3.1.1(3), Lean constructs a continuous homomorphic section of the S-idelic modulus. The radial Mellin integral has an integrable exponential majorant on every closed strip in the right half-plane, uniformly on bounded families of S-local tests. Lean verifies absolute convergence, holomorphy, and compact-uniform convergence of the log-modulus truncations. The logarithmic modulus and norm-one class give a product coordinate system for the canonical section. Pushforward of Lebesgue measure times norm-one Haar is Haar, and Fubini identifies the quotient theta Mellin integral with the radial integral on the right half-plane. Weil normalization with counting measure on the S-units relates every regular multiplicative Haar measure to a product quotient measure. Lean proves the idelic unfolding, a common integrable majorant on each closed positive strip, holomorphy there, and continuity in the test. The two section-dependent character factors cancel in the radial integral. The local Tate factors and functional equation remain open. [SLocalThetaMellin.lean](SLocalThetaMellin.lean) [SLocalZetaUnfolding.lean](SLocalZetaUnfolding.lean)

The `8ce8212` refresh supplies a verified smooth-case proof for Remark B.4.1.4 and the geometric estimate for the local Dirac comb. The local port proves summability of the smooth Mellin samples and extends the Dirac argument to strong-dual convergence. The convergence direction of (C.2.3.16) now checks on the actual projective tensor completion. The later `93dbe47` refresh supplies the converse, verified below. The port of the reciprocal gamma bounds also gives a verified exponential strip bound on the reciprocal of the actual archimedean Euler product. The numbered result count excludes these remarks and partial results. For Proposition 2.1.4.1, the canonical elementary tensor map now gives a continuous linear equivalence between the inductive tensor of the archimedean Schwartz space with the locally constant compactly supported tests on the finite product, and the S-local image. The binary product of finite test factors now also has its canonical continuous linear equivalence with the inductive tensor product. The finite iteration and remaining structural clauses were verified in the later completion entry. [SLocalBruhatProjections.lean](SLocalBruhatProjections.lean) · [LocallyConstantProduct.lean](LocallyConstantProduct.lean)

For Lemma 3.3.2.2, Lean writes every finite local zeta integral as a finite shell sum plus a geometric tail. Averaging over the units eliminates the tail for ramified characters. On the unramified cylinder the only distribution-valued pole is simple and lies at the trivial quasi-character. Its residue on a test $f$ is $\mu_v(\mathcal O_v^\times) f(0)/\log q_v$. [FiniteLocalZeta.lean](FiniteLocalZeta.lean) · [G92](OLD_GAPS.MD#g92-the-finite-place-residue-has-an-extra-euler-factor)

The external refresh supplies a Gaussian estimate and an exponent-reindexing argument. Both now check in our positive-real decay space. The exponent shift extends to complex powers, and the positive-real case of the intermediate-weight inclusion of Lemma D.3.3.2 also checks. The general modulated-group extension remains open. [MellinBergman.lean](MellinBergman.lean)

Lemma 5.3.1.3 now checks for every finite completion. The integral-unit averages give the trivial-isotype projection, and Fourier completeness of the compact unit group gives uniqueness on the entire test space. Lemma D.1.6.18 now checks on the elementary Schwartz spaces. The compact quotient admits an elementary presentation. Proposition D.1.8.3 now checks in full. Convolution with nonnegative product local units gives an approximate identity at the level of each continuous functional on compact tests. In Lemma 3.3.1.7(1), the actual S-local zeta integrals in the norm-one character charts now have Schwartz vertical traces, bounded in every Schwartz seminorm on a positive closed strip. Iterating the operator and using the existing two-endpoint majorant bounds the weighted logarithmic profiles in Schwartz space. Fourier transformation transfers those bounds to the vertical traces. For Lemma 3.3.1.7(3), Lean restricts the standard adelic character to each finite completion and proves that the annihilator of the integer ring is a valuation ball of radius $q^{-n}$. It constructs additive Haar measure with integer-ring mass $q^{n/2}$ and verifies the Fourier transform and double transform on the integer-ring test from Example A.3.1.10. Corrected Theorem 5.2.4.1 now checks on the cofinal stages containing the different. For Theorem 5.2.1.1, the quotient topology has the verified locally convex final property, and every compatible family of continuous linear maps from the S-local theta quotients has a unique continuous extension from the global quotient. The printed coordinate projection fails to preserve the global theta kernel even at the archimedean stage, for every number field. A test minus its principal translate has zero theta. Its projected translate is an archimedean dilation, and theta decay at large modulus contradicts invariance under that dilation. Lean proves that the proposed projection cannot descend even as a function on the quotient. The strict stage embeddings and the remaining strict LF and dual clauses are open. The numbered register stands at 410 of 598. [Theta quotient topology](SLocalThetaTopology.lean)

For the stage embeddings in Theorem 5.2.1.1, Lean checks a replacement for point evaluation under a one-place enlargement. The difference between evaluations at the inserted idele and at its inverse-uniformizer shift recovers the smaller-stage evaluation. Both evaluations are continuous on the larger quotient. Lean also groups arbitrary summable larger-stage unit series by new-place valuation and identifies each fiber with a translate of the smaller unit group. For an arbitrary finite Bruhat test, the two corresponding slices agree near zero and vanish outside a compact set in the added coordinate. Their difference therefore has finite support in the integer valuation index. The remaining step is to assemble the translated slices as a continuous map on the test spaces and show it gives a retraction on the theta quotients. The register remains 410 of 598. Lean now verifies the continuous retraction for each one-place enlargement in Theorem 5.2.1.1. It averages over the new finite place’s compact integral units, which removes the rotations caused by smaller-stage units and preserves the theta kernel. The signed slices then produce a continuous map on the test spaces. Its theta orbit sum is the difference of the two larger-stage evaluations, giving the left inverse after passage to the quotients. The one-place range is closed, and composition gives a closed embedding for every inclusion of finite stages. The global embeddings and the structural and strong-dual conclusions remain open. The register is 410 of 598. [Stage retractions](SLocalThetaStageRetraction.lean)

The global embeddings in Theorem 5.2.1.1 now check. Along a countable cofinal tower, successive finite-stage retractions compose compatibly and extend to the global quotient by its locally convex universal property. Every finite theta stage is a complemented closed subspace of the global quotient. Lean also verifies that bounded subsets come from bounded subsets in a single stage and that the strong dual has the topology of the compatible stage restrictions. The global and every S-local strong dual are complete. The completeness proof tests continuity of a limiting linear form on the first-countable archimedean Schwartz slices. The strict presentations by nuclear Fréchet spaces and the remaining completeness, nuclearity and Montel claims still need proofs. The register is 410 of 598. For Proposition 2.1.4.1 and Theorem 5.2.1.1, the countable basis of the finite test factor gives a strict, complemented tower of finite products of archimedean Schwartz space inside the actual S-local test topology. Quotienting each stage by the restriction of the theta kernel gives Fréchet spaces and a final presentation of the actual S-local theta quotient. For finite places, Lean also verifies that S-unit translations leave theta classes fixed and reduce valuation vectors to a finite box bounded by the class number. For the same strictness step, Lean now gives a finite-place shell decomposition. For a contracting local unit, every locally constant compactly supported test is a finite sum of dilates of tests on one compact annulus, plus a dilated unit-ball indicator. Any finite family of tests admits a common open subgroup of integral units fixing it. At a fixed subgroup, the normalized annulus test space is finite-dimensional, by a finite cover of the annulus by open unit orbits. Lean also constructs an S-unit contracting at any prescribed finite place of the stage and of norm one at all other finite places. The normalized annulus spaces are invariant under these norm-one twists. The remaining strictness step is to assemble the finite-place normal forms, transfer the S-unit translations to the archimedean factor in the theta quotient, and verify the intrinsic Fréchet topology of the resulting fixed subspaces. For Theorem 5.2.1.1, the local shell normal forms now combine on the actual theta quotient. Lean factors each principal S-unit pullback into its archimedean Schwartz pullback and the individual finite-place twists. The shell powers are removed one coordinate at a time. Twists at other coordinates have norm one and preserve the normalized template spaces. Algebraic projections onto invariant factors show that finite tests with coupled coordinates lie in the span of separated invariant tests. For any specified tuple of open integral-unit subgroups, the resulting invariant test span in the theta quotient is exactly the range of a continuous linear map from a finite product of archimedean Schwartz spaces. These spans exhaust the quotient. The induced topology on each range still has to be compared with its intrinsic Fréchet quotient topology, and a countable strict tower has to be chosen. The primal nuclearity of the elementary and full Schwartz–Bruhat spaces now checks. Lean verifies the separable nuclear Fréchet conclusion of Theorem 5.4.1.1(2). In a first-countable nuclear space, compact seminorm images have separable linear spans. For Theorem 5.3.2.3, Lean verifies coordinate-plane restriction in both test topologies and proves that the claimed exact coefficient orders fail even for a first derivative of the Dirac distribution on the line. The coordinate-plane proof now identifies the finite normal jets that determine the distribution. A rescaled normal cutoff makes every derivative through the distribution order tend to zero on a fixed compact support when those jets vanish. Symmetry of the iterated derivatives and differentiation along the plane show that vanishing of the pure normal jets suffices. On compactly supported tests on the real line, monomials times a fixed bump interpolate the finite jet and give the derivative-evaluation expansion for every finite-order distribution carried by the origin. The general coefficient bounds in Theorem 5.3.2.3 and the Schwartz jet topology in Theorem 5.3.2.1 remain open. The register stays at 483. For Theorem 5.4.1.1 and Proposition 5.4.1.4, the selected additive Haar measure gives full measure to the S-ideles. For Theorem 3.3.1.1 and Lemma 3.3.1.7, the finite local Fourier equation now checks on all locally constant compactly supported tests, for every unitary phase. For Lemma 3.3.1.7(3)(a)(i)–(ii), Lean verifies the explicit real and complex Tate equations on the entire local Schwartz test spaces. With the positive adelic Fourier kernel the Gaussian reference transforms by $i$ in odd real parity, and by $i^{|m|}$ with reversal of the complex angular mode. The two equations therefore have epsilon constants $(-i)^a$ in real parity $a\bmod 2$ and $(-i)^{|m|}$ in complex mode $m$. The complex trace kernel uses twice Euclidean volume and frequency $2\overline y$. The strip identities extend with their explicit Gamma ratios as meromorphic germs at every point. Corrected Lemma 3.3.1.7 now checks on the S-local zeta spaces. The inverse-phase unit-shell test has constant Mellin value equal to the unit Haar mass. Its Fourier transform is supported on the shell of norm $q^{d+n}$, with $d$ the different exponent and $n$ the positive least multiplicative conductor exponent. Integrating over that shell determines the Tate multiplier. The coefficient is the finite sum over any system of congruence cosets, with mass $q^{-n}$ times the additive Tamagawa mass of the integer ring. Lean verifies that (3.3.1.30) omits $n$ from the spectral exponent. The transitive axiom audit passed and the register is 489. For Theorem 3.4.3.3(1), the real and complex Gamma-normalized local families are now holomorphic in the strong Schwartz dual on the entire parameter plane. For Theorem 3.4.3.3(2), Lean now verifies an entire family in the strong adelic Schwartz–Bruhat dual. One scaled adelic reference normalizes the family at every parameter. The meromorphic factorization and the idelic dilation law hold also at exceptional parameters in the stated germ and pointwise senses, respectively. The numerator on a pure stage test splits as meromorphic germs into a cleared archimedean integral and the individual cleared finite integrals. For Theorem 3.4.3.3(2), Lean now verifies the separated-tensor formula at every exponent. The archimedean integral splits by Fubini, and the entire continuation gives the product of the individually normalized real, complex, and finite distributions, including at exceptional parameters. Padding the unnormalized numerator multiplies it by exactly the newly selected finite integral-unit masses. Theorem 1.7.1.1 now checks in all three clauses. Distinct residue primes give at most one common finite Euler pole. Infinitely many poles at one parameter require two distinct residue primes because each rational prime has only finitely many places above it. For the algebraic norm-twist case, Lean derives the needed four-exponentials implication from the stated Schanuel hypothesis. The finite-order case uses the root-of-unity powers and has no conjectural hypothesis. For Theorem 7.1.3.2(3)(b), Lean now applies the Schanuel implication to the meromorphic S-local Tate ratio. The actual norm-twist factorization supplies the finite algebraicity data from the unramified uniformizer values of the untwisted character. Counting by residue-prime fibres shows that an order above twice the degree needs three distinct rational primes if the archimedean count is zero. Lean proves rational linear independence of any finite family of logarithms of prime powers with distinct bases by applying rational valuations after exponentiating an integer relation. With the common archimedean shift stated explicitly, the twice-degree bound reduces to the complex Six Exponentials Theorem. The supporting proofs passed the transitive axiom audit. For Theorem 7.1.3.2(3)(b), Lean now derives the common archimedean imaginary shift from the real sign and integral power and the two complex integral powers of the untwisted infinity type. The embedded positive coordinates have norm $e^t$. At a complex place the coordinate itself is $e^{t/2}$, so the radial exponent is half the sum of the two integral powers. Differentiation at the identity identifies each local exponent as the radial exponent plus the norm twist. Both pole bounds apply to the same meromorphic Tate multiplier that gives the S-local Fourier functional equation for every test. For Theorem 7.1.3.2(3)(b), Lean proves that every unitary S-local character is represented by a purely imaginary exponent in the canonical chart. For Lemma 9.3.2.5, Lean verifies that convolution in logarithmic coordinates on the positive-real group corresponds to pointwise multiplication through the full scalar Fourier–Mellin equivalence. For Proposition 3.2.1.3, Lean now verifies that the completed Euler continuation is entire on every nontrivial norm-one component and has exactly two simple poles on the canonical trivial component. The corrected Proposition 3.2.1.3 now checks in the original spectral parameter of an arbitrary unitary idele-class character. Lean proves the unique height, identifies how the actual finite Euler factors and the archimedean weights change under the shift, and checks independence of the finite stage used for the archimedean factors. The assembled theorem verifies entireness, the shifted simple poles with their corrected residues, reflection for the inverse character, and zero exclusion. For Lemma B.1.1.2, Lean combines the finite functional equation, the bounded Euler product on the right, the Gamma recurrence on negative half-integer lines, and interpolation of the pole-cleared continuation. The unique character height transports this estimate to the original spectral parameter. For Remark B.1.1.4, Cauchy estimates on a wider strip verify polynomial growth for all holomorphic derivatives. Lean also checks the missing chain-rule signs in the differentiated reflection formula. Remarks are outside the 598-result denominator. For Proposition B.1.1.3, the sharp archimedean exponent is obtained by interpolating the exact recurrence degrees on the two boundary lines of real parts $-1/2$ and $1/2$. A second interpolation gives the finite L-function convexity bound with the correct critical-strip exponent. Lemma B.1.1.2 supplies the arbitrary fixed-strip polynomial estimate used in (B.6.1.38). The transitive axiom audit passed. [Finite Hecke growth](FiniteHeckeGrowth.lean) · [Global Tate reflection](GlobalHeckeTateReflection.lean) · [Character chart](ManuscriptHeckeCharacterChart.lean)

For Theorem B.6.1.6, Lean applies the three-factor Euler-product inequality at a point slightly to the right of one. The squared character is evaluated at twice the height. A horizontal derivative estimate controls the change back to the line of real part one, and the Dedekind-zeta simple-pole bound controls the trivial factor. The proof transports the lower bound to the original character parameter and passes the transitive axiom audit. [Finite Hecke reciprocal](FiniteHeckeReciprocal.lean)

For Theorem B.6.1.3, Lean applies Landau's lemma on disks centered at $2+it$. The finite Euler inequality gives a uniform lower bound at the center, and the verified polynomial strip bound controls the normalized function over each disk. The derivative of the band width controls how it changes between nearby ordinates, yielding separation from each nearby zero. The result retains the zero-free hypothesis and passes the transitive axiom audit. [Landau's lemma](LandauDisk.lean) · [Finite Hecke logarithmic derivative](FiniteHeckeLogDerivative.lean)

For Theorem B.6.1.7, Lean compares the finite multiplier with the Euler product over the complement and constructs its continuation. Jensen's estimate counts the zeros with multiplicity, and the Blaschke factorization gives a lower bound in terms of their logarithmic distances. Each positive logarithmic loss has integral two on the real line. The weighted integral controls a single exceptional set for the whole horizontal segment. For Corollary B.6.1.8, a first-derivative bound gives small disks on which the lower bound persists. Cauchy's estimate for the reciprocal then gives the derivative bounds on the selected two-sided sequence. The transitive axiom audit passed for both results. For Corollary B.6.1.5, Lean proves that the norm of the three-factor Euler product decreases with the real part, then differentiates to obtain the logarithmic-derivative inequality. The one-sided Landau estimate at the doubled ordinate, the term of a possible nearby zero at the original ordinate, and the Dedekind-zeta pole bound yield a zero-free band of width proportional to $1/\log |t|$ at large heights. A second disk estimate proves the line-one bound, and the character height transports it to the original spectral parameter. The transitive axiom audit passed. The initial band argument allowed its width constant to depend on the character and field. [Hecke zero-free band and line-one bound](FiniteHeckeZeroFree.lean)

For corrected Proposition B.6.1.1, Lean retains the explicit field-degree exponent in polynomial growth on the fixed strip of real parts from $-6$ to $10$. This controls the Landau bound with an absolute numerator. The set of nonpositive meromorphic order is open near the whole line of real part one, including poles. Compactness covers the remaining bounded heights by enlarging the scale, and translation handles imaginary norm twists. Meromorphic order is independent of the choice of continuation. Lean also verifies that any nonreal positive-order zero of the trivial continuation becomes a real zero of a nonquadratic norm character at the same real part. The region and this obstruction passed the transitive axiom audit. [Zero-free region](FiniteHeckeZeroFree.lean) · [Norm-twist obstruction](HeckeZeroFreeObstruction.lean)

For Lemma D.1.6.19(3), Lean verifies that probability Haar averaging over the compact kernel becomes restriction to its annihilator under Fourier transform. The cutoff to the open upper subgroup becomes probability Haar averaging over its compact annihilator. These identities apply to continuous integrable functions, including on groups with Haar measure that is not sigma-finite. Lean also verifies the corrected inverse formula with the transform of the upper-subgroup cutoff and the restriction of the ambient Plancherel measure. The transitive axiom audit passed. Lemma D.1.6.19(1) now checks for every invariant differential operator on a Hausdorff locally compact abelian group. Algebra induction yields the symbol formula and a continuous operator on the full Schwartz–Bruhat space. For clause (2), Lean verifies the dual-stage pairing and support and invariance, expresses the integral on the stage quotients, and bounds the transform with arbitrary real and lattice coordinate polynomials. Corrected Lemma D.1.6.19 now checks in full. Multiplication by a real phase coordinate preserves the physical elementary Schwartz space. Scalar Fourier evaluations are continuous by the stage L¹ seminorm, and the closed graph argument gives continuity on each pair of stages. For Theorem D.1.7.1(1), Lean proves pointwise distributional Poisson summation for every Schwartz–Bruhat test and arbitrary closed subgroup. Uniform bounds for the Haar mass in translated unit boxes give absolute convergence and continuity of the fibre integrals. Applied to the Fourier-transformed test, the same bounds prove integrability on the annihilator and remove the integrability premise in the earlier Weil formula. For B.7.1.2, Lean now verifies the residues of the actual finite Hecke continuation inside the translated zero-free rectangle. The left width uses the maximum ordinate $|\Im s|+T$. The distinct-pole calculation gives the log derivative at $s$ plus the explicit exponential pole contribution with the signs in (B.7.1.8). At coincidence it gives $\log x$ plus the regular value of the pole-subtracted derivative. Corrected Theorem B.7.1.2 is now complete. Lean identifies the shifted continuation with a continuation of the Dedekind zeta function on the pure norm-twist component. The resulting remainder tends to zero uniformly on compact subsets over the admissible cutoffs, including compacts containing the pole. At the pole, rounding the cutoff down proves $\log x + O(1)$ for all sufficiently large real $x$. The ideal-indexed von Mangoldt sum equals the prime-power polynomial, by uniqueness of the prime-power presentation and vanishing on all other ideals. Lean now identifies the conductor term of B.4.2.2. At a finite place the multiplier exponent is the different exponent plus the least depth of the unit filtration on which the character is trivial. Only finitely many depths are nonzero. The norm of the product ideal gives $|D_F|\,N\mathfrak f_\chi$, and a norm twist preserves the unit depths and changes only the constant of the reflection multiplier. The logarithmic derivative in the manuscript's height chart therefore has the verified conductor term. Jensen's inequality bounds the finite-function zero multiplicities in moving disks at high ordinates, and Landau's estimate controls the logarithmic derivative there in terms of the separation from those zeros. For B.4.4.9, Lean computes the Mellin symbol of the actual Hecke character along the archimedean exponential. The real and complex radial symbols are the local radial parameters plus the abscissa and the shift $-it$. The complex normalization uses half-speed in the ordinary modulus so that the local log norm has speed one. The complex angular symbol is the integral angle index times $i$, and the norm-section symbol at real abscissa $a$ is $a-it$. The exact evaluation holds for every mixed archimedean velocity. Every global invariant direction has such a velocity, and the same velocity maps to the elementary stage by its surjective linear map. Nine declarations passed the transitive axiom audit. For B.4.4.9, Lean now proves a polynomial bound for the number of distinct surviving norm-one Hecke characters for each actual compact Bruhat test. The test's invariance kernel gives a compact quotient of norm-one classes. Each unit torus direction lifts from that quotient to a fixed archimedean velocity. The character along this lift has phase $2\pi$ times its integer torus frequency. Taking norms in the verified symbol formula bounds that frequency by $CT$ for an archimedean parameter box of radius $T$. The resulting bound on the number of norm-one restrictions is $M(2\lceil CT\rceil+1)^n$, where $n$ is the torus dimension and $M$ is the size of the finite dual. The kernel in this count has a compactly generated global annihilator, so the conductor bounds apply to the same family. [Character count](IdelicHeckeFrequencyBoxes.lean)

For Proposition 5.4.1.4, Lean proves that the left-hand pole conditions are closed and characterize entireness of the right component for every matching pair. Corrected Theorem 5.4.1.1 now checks. Multiplication by the full finite Euler denominator leaves only archimedean principal parts in an arbitrary matching pair. Their sum belongs to the finite Weil image, so dividing through the corresponding operator on tests recovers the original pair. Open mapping gives the ordinary closed embedding, and vertical translation proves it for every norm section. Remark D.3.3.6 now checks for general modulated groups in the inductive topology. For Proposition D.3.3.10, one bounded weighted image places the entire family in a common structural quotient. Lean now verifies Proposition D.3.3.10(1)–(2) and the corrected test in (3). The corrected test uses a countable set of abscissae that brackets every element of the interval, with equality allowed. Adding the least and greatest points when they belong to the interval supplies such a set. On a fixed quotient, the bracketing twists determine the topology by the two logarithmic half-space estimates. The stage is separable nuclear Fréchet, and every bounded set lies in one stage with its intrinsic topology. The tensor comparisons remain open. For Theorem D.3.3.7(2) and (4), Lean constructs a monotone cofinal sequence of whole-group stages, proves barrelledness from the Fréchet stages, and proves that bounded linear maps into locally convex spaces are continuous. The twist at zero is the continuous injective inclusion into the full Schwartz–Bruhat space. These declarations use only standard Lean axioms. [Decay topology](ModulatedDecayTopology.lean)

Lean verifies the countable strict Fréchet presentation in Theorem D.3.3.7(2) and the completeness, nuclearity and Montel property of the actual decay space. The strong dual in part (3) identifies with compatible families of strong-dual restrictions on the stages. Every bounded set is covered by one stage, so the strong topology agrees with the topology of this closed subspace of a product. The strong dual is consequently complete, nuclear and Montel. The compact-generation claim in Theorem D.3.3.7(2) now checks. Compact generation of the kernel dual gives finite-dimensional local charts for the kernel by Pontryagin biduality. Taking the product with the logarithmic real coordinate gives such charts on the whole group, so its dual is compactly generated. The structural stage with trivial kernel then exhausts the decay space and gives its Fréchet topology. [Nuclear Fréchet case](ModulatedDecayFrechet.lean)

The compact-interval topology assertion of Theorem D.3.3.7(4) now checks. The sum of the two endpoint twists is a continuous linear equivalence onto the full Schwartz–Bruhat space. Its inverse divides by the sum of the endpoint weights. Each normalized endpoint weight is a smooth logistic function of the log coordinate with bounded derivatives, which preserves each elementary Schwartz stage. The intermediate twists are continuous by bracketing, and the endpoint sum factors through the tuple of twists. This proves equality of the projective and inductive topologies on every nonempty compact interval. Parts (2)–(4) now check in full, and the tensor isomorphisms in (1) remain open. [Compact-interval topology](ModulatedDecayCompactInterval.lean)

For Theorem D.3.3.7(1), Lean constructs a whole-group structural stage with the prescribed angular kernel. For C.5.1.30(4), Euclidean Schwartz currying now checks in both directions. Weak directional derivatives of a Schwartz-valued Schwartz function again satisfy the scalar Schwartz tests. Differentiation in either variable preserves the reverse evaluation family. Joint continuity and rapid decay of the values applied to that family yield bounds for all mixed derivatives. For Lemma 5.3.1.5, Lean now verifies both ordinary theta closed embeddings over the rational field for every finite selection of places. Every angular theta class there has an open norm-one stabilizer. Indeed, a norm-one representative with positive real coordinate and finite components in the chosen angular level has real coordinate one. Its nonzero components therefore have characters factoring through a finite quotient. Finite synthesis proves closedness on the S-local quotient, the angular Fréchet structure gives the inverse topology, and the finite-stage retractions give the global closed embedding. All five audited declarations use only standard Lean axioms. General number fields remain open, so the register stays at 550 of 598.
